# 对照表 · 第二册 · 残响与阵营事件

共 698 条，已译 0 条。**【原文】是定位用的，不要动。**
【新文】留空＝还没译，装载时这一条回落中文。
中文原文改了而这里的【原文】没跟上时，`python3 langpack.py stale <lang>` 会报出来。

### [阵-001] echo_dream · 事件正文
【原文】
【残响】午夜，你梦见一双不属于你的手在拆一台引擎。每个零件的名字
你都叫得出来——用一种你没学过的口音。
醒来时，你的两根手指还捏着被角，正在拧一颗不存在的螺栓。
【新文】
ECHO — At midnight you dream of a pair of hands that are not yours taking an engine apart. You can name every part
of it—in an accent you never learned.
When you wake, two of your fingers are still pinching the corner of the blanket, turning a bolt that is not there.

### [阵-002] echo_dream · 选项1
【原文】
顺着梦，把那台引擎画下来。
【新文】
Follow the dream and draw that engine.

### [阵-003] echo_dream · 选项1 成功
【原文】
图纸完成的瞬间你认出来了：这是上个时代的心肺机。前世的你修过很多台。知识淌回手指，像河认出故道。
【新文】
The moment the drawing is finished you recognize it: it is a heart-lung machine from the last era. In an earlier life you repaired a great many of them. The knowledge flows back into your fingers, the way a river recognizes its old bed.

【新文】
The lines fight each other on the paper. The memory is somebody else’s, the hands are yours, and there is a whole death in between.

压下去。这不是你的记忆。

### [阵-006] echo_dream · 选项2 成功
【原文】
你学会了在残响涌上来时数呼吸的节奏。它们退回去了，像潮水记住了堤。
【新文】
You learn to count the rhythm of your breathing when the echoes come up. They pull back, the way a tide remembers the embankment.

越压越响。第二天你在早餐桌上脱口说出一个没人听过的机油牌子。

### [阵-009] echo_dream · 选项3 结果
【原文】
梦讲了一整夜。你现在知道自己为什么总在黄昏时感到怀念，那是前世换班的时间。
【新文】
The dream talks all night. Now you know why you always feel homesick at dusk: that was the hour an earlier life changed shift.

### [阵-010] echo_dream · 事件正文（变体1）
【原文】
【残响】这一次不是引擎。
你梦见自己在给一件很小的东西上油——小到要眯起眼睛，小到那双手在梦里也发抖。
你不知道那是什么零件，但你知道下一步该往哪个方向拧，也知道拧过头会怎么响。
醒来时枕头上有一小块油渍。你昨晚没碰过任何机器。
【新文】
ECHO — This time it is not an engine.
You dream of oiling something very small—small enough that you have to squint, small enough that the hands are shaking even in the dream.
You do not know what the part is, but you know which way to turn next, and you know what it sounds like when you go a turn too far.
When you wake there is a small oil stain on the pillow. You did not touch a machine last night.

### [阵-011] echo_dream · 选项1（变体1）
【原文】
去五金摊，把梦里那个手感一件一件对过去。
【新文】
Go to the hardware stall and try that feel against one part after another.

### [阵-012] echo_dream · 选项1 成功（变体1）
【原文】
第十一件对上了。摊主说这是老式助听器的音圈，早停产了，现在只有一种人还在修。
你问是哪种人。他说：「聋子的儿子。」
【新文】
The eleventh one matches. The stallholder says it is the voice coil out of an old-style hearing aid, long out of production; only one kind of person still repairs them.
You ask which kind. He says: “A deaf man’s son.”

### [阵-013] echo_dream · 选项1 失败（变体1）

你摸了一下午，什么也没对上。回家的路上你一直在搓手指，像还在找那个手感。
【新文】
You handle parts all afternoon and nothing matches. On the way home you keep rubbing your fingers together, as though you are still looking for the feel of it.

### [阵-014] echo_dream · 选项2（变体1）
【原文】
不去找。让它自己再来一次。
【新文】

### [阵-015] echo_dream · 选项2 成功（变体1）
【原文】
第三个晚上它又来了，这次更长，也更慢。你没有醒，一直看到那双手把东西装了回去。
醒来时你什么也没记住，只记得装回去的那一下很轻。
【新文】
On the third night it comes back, longer this time, and slower. You do not wake, and you watch until those hands have put the thing back together.
When you wake you remember none of it, only that putting it back took a very light touch.

### [阵-016] echo_dream · 选项2 失败（变体1）
【原文】
它没有再来。你连着守了六个晚上，什么也没等到。
第七个晚上你梦见了自己的手，普普通通的，什么也不会。
【新文】
It does not come back. You keep watch six nights running and nothing arrives.
On the seventh night you dream of your own hands, ordinary, able to do nothing at all.

### [阵-018] echo_dream · 选项3 结果（变体1）
【原文】
枕套洗了两遍才干净。晾在阳台上的时候你站着看了一会儿。
那块油渍原来的位置，正好是你睡觉时脸颊挨着的地方。
【新文】
The pillowcase takes two washes to come clean. Hanging it on the balcony you stand and look at it a while.
Where the oil stain had been is exactly where your cheek rests when you sleep.

### [阵-019] echo_dream · 事件正文（变体2）
【原文】
【残响】你在梦里认出了这是梦——因为这双手你已经借过太多次了。
它们照旧在忙，照旧不理你。这一次你没有跟着它们看，你抬起头，
想看看这双手连着的是谁。
梦里的光只够照亮手腕。再往上是没有画完的地方。
【新文】
ECHO — In the dream you recognize that it is a dream—because you have borrowed these hands too many times.
They are busy as always, ignoring you as always. This time you do not follow what they are doing. You lift your head,
wanting to see who these hands are attached to.
The light in the dream reaches as far as the wrists. Above that is the part that was never drawn.

### [阵-020] echo_dream · 选项1（变体2）
【原文】
开口问：「你是谁。」
【新文】
Speak: “Who are you.”

### [阵-021] echo_dream · 选项1 结果（变体2）
【原文】
手停了。整个梦停了，像一段被按住的录音。
然后它继续拧那颗螺栓，从刚才停下的地方，一格都没差。
醒来之后你想了很久：不回答，和答不上来，在梦里是同一件事。
【新文】
The hands stop. The whole dream stops, like a recording held under a finger.
Then it goes on turning that bolt, from exactly where it stopped, not one notch off.
After waking you think about it a long time: not answering, and having no answer, are the same thing inside a dream.

【新文】

### [阵-023] echo_dream · 选项2 成功（变体2）
【原文】
四只手在一颗螺栓上，力道居然对得上。拧到最后半圈它松开了，让你自己拧完。
醒来时你的手腕酸得厉害，像真的干过一夜活。
【新文】
Four hands on one bolt, and the pressure actually matches. At the last half turn they let go and let you finish it yourself.
When you wake your wrist aches badly, as though you really had worked all night.

### [阵-024] echo_dream · 选项2 失败（变体2）

剩下的半夜你睡得很沉，什么也没有。
【新文】
The moment you touch them the dream scatters, like a film of oil blown off water.
You sleep heavily for the rest of the night, and there is nothing in it.

### [阵-025] echo_dream · 选项3（变体2）

不看了。转身，在梦里走开。

### [阵-026] echo_dream · 选项3 结果（变体2）
【原文】
你背对着那双手，一直走到梦的边上。身后的动静一直没停。
醒来时天还没亮。你躺着，听见自己房间里什么声音也没有，然后才想起来那本来就该是什么声音也没有。
【新文】
With your back to those hands you walk all the way to the edge of the dream. The sound behind you never stops.
When you wake it is not light yet. You lie there and hear that there is no sound at all in your room, and only then remember that there was never supposed to be.

### [阵-027] echo_slip · 事件正文
【原文】
【残响】聚餐上有人抱怨假肢接口发炎。你头也不抬：「Ⅳ型接口就该配硅凝胶垫圈，
原厂那个是成本削减的产物。」
满桌寂静，坐你对面的人慢慢放下了筷子。
【新文】
ECHO — At dinner somebody complains that a prosthetic interface has gone septic. Without looking up you say: “A type IV interface should be running silicone gaskets.
The factory item is what cost-cutting produced.”
The table goes silent. The person across from you slowly sets their chopsticks down.

### [阵-036] echo_slip_pro · 事件正文
【原文】
【残响】聚餐上有人抱怨假肢接口发炎。你头也不抬：「Ⅳ型接口就该配硅凝胶垫圈，
原厂那个是成本削减的产物。」
满桌人转过来——不是警惕，是兴奋。「你哪个厂出来的？」
你答不上来。你从没进过厂。这句话是从别人嘴里穿过死亡走到你舌头上的。
【新文】
ECHO — At dinner somebody complains that a prosthetic interface has gone septic. Without looking up you say: “A type IV interface should be running silicone gaskets.
The factory item is what cost-cutting produced.”
The whole table turns to you—not wary. Excited. “Which plant did you come out of?”
You have no answer. You have never been inside a plant. That sentence walked out of somebody else’s mouth, through a death, and onto your tongue.

### [阵-045] harbor_cargo · 事件正文
【原文】
凌晨三点，码头。雾浓得像纱布裹在脸上。
接头人把一只军绿色防水箱推到你脚边。箱子很沉，里面的东西轻轻撞击箱壁，
发出骨头碰骨头的闷响——不对，是金属碰金属。
「六副，都验过了。」接头人往手心呵了口气，「原主的数据清过了，理论上。」
他强调了「理论上」三个字。

你打开箱子。六副二手义肢，按大小码放在防潮泡沫里。
最上面那只右手的食指还微微蜷着，保持着握笔的弧度。
【新文】
Three in the morning, the docks. The fog is thick as gauze wrapped over your face.
The contact pushes an army-green waterproof crate up against your foot. It is heavy, and what is inside knocks softly against the walls,
a dull sound like bone on bone—no. Metal on metal.
“Six sets, all checked.” The contact breathes into his cupped hands. “Original owners’ data has been wiped. In theory.”
He leans on in theory.

You open the crate. Six secondhand prosthetics, laid out by size in anti-damp foam.
The index finger of the right hand on top is still slightly curled, holding the shape of a grip on a pen.

### [阵-046] harbor_cargo · 选项1
【原文】
逐只检查接口编号，核对来源。
【新文】
Check every interface number one by one and verify where they came from.

### [阵-047] harbor_cargo · 选项1 成功
【原文】
编号后四位对上了灰港的回收登记簿。六副义肢，三副来自自愿置换——升级换代，
旧的折价。两副来自太平间——死者家属签了捐赠协议，或者签了长得像捐赠协议的东西。
最后一副没有编号。你翻遍了整只手臂，在肘关节内侧找到一排被锉掉的字。
锉痕很新。
【新文】
The last four digits match Ashport’s recovery register. Six prosthetics: three from voluntary replacement—upgrades,
the old ones traded in. Two from a morgue—the families signed a donation agreement, or signed something that looked a lot like one.
The last has no number at all. You go over the whole arm and find, on the inside of the elbow, a row of characters that have been filed away.
The file marks are fresh.

### [阵-048] harbor_cargo · 选项1 失败
【原文】
编号簿的字迹被海风和湿气泡得发糊。你只对上了两副。
剩下四副，你闭着眼签了收。箱子关上的时候，那只蜷着的手指像是动了一下。
【新文】
The writing in the register has gone soft with sea wind and damp. You match only two.
The other four you sign for with your eyes shut. As the crate closes, that curled finger seems to move.

### [阵-049] harbor_cargo · 选项2

【新文】

### [阵-050] harbor_cargo · 选项2 结果
【原文】
你不是第一次接货了。有些事情，不查比查了好——查了，你就得对结果负责。
你在签收单上按了手印。墨迹和雾气混在一起，指纹模糊得像别人的。
【新文】
This is not your first delivery. Some things are better not checked—once you check, you are answerable for what you find.
You press your thumbprint onto the receipt. The ink runs into the fog and the print blurs until it could be somebody else’s.

### [阵-051] harbor_cargo · 选项3

【新文】

### [阵-052] harbor_cargo · 选项3 成功
【原文】
接头人盯着你看了三秒，像在判断你是不是新来的。
「去哪了？」他把烟头按灭在箱盖上，「这行有个规矩：零件过手，名字落水。
码头下面的海水含盐量特别高，什么都能腐蚀干净。」
他拍了拍箱子：「你拿到的是零件，不是人。零件没有去向。」
【新文】
The contact studies you for three seconds, judging whether you are new.
“Where did they go?” He puts his cigarette out on the crate lid. “There’s a rule in this trade: parts pass through hands, names go in the water.
The seawater under this dock has a very high salt content. It corrodes anything clean.”
He pats the crate. “What you have there is parts, not people. Parts don’t have destinations.”

### [阵-053] harbor_cargo · 选项3 失败
【原文】
接头人笑了一下，那种见过太多新人的笑。
「小孩，你搬就是了。」他拎起箱子的另一头，帮你一起抬上了板车。
有些问题的答案和没有答案一样重。
【新文】
The contact gives a small laugh, the laugh of a man who has seen too many new people.
“Kid, just carry it.” He picks up the other end of the crate and helps you get it onto the handcart.
Some questions have answers that weigh the same as no answer at all.

### [阵-054] harbor_cargo · 回响1
【原文】
接头人看见你就把烟掐了。「老规矩，不验不问。」他顿了一下，
「不过今天这批……你自己看吧。有一副手的指纹，登记簿上查不到主人。」
【新文】
The contact puts his cigarette out as soon as he sees you. “Same rule. Don’t check, don’t ask.” He pauses.
“Though this batch… have a look yourself. One of the hands has fingerprints the register can’t find an owner for.”

### [阵-055] harbor_cargo · 回响2
【原文】
今天接头人带了个新人来。十六七岁，手插在袖子里，一直在看别处。
接头人没有介绍你，只朝箱子偏了偏头，然后退开两步，把位置让给了你。
新人小声问了一句什么。接头人说：「问他。」
【新文】
The contact has brought somebody new today. Sixteen, seventeen, hands up his sleeves, looking anywhere else.
The contact does not introduce you; he only tilts his head at the crate, then steps back two paces and leaves the place to you.
The new one asks something under his breath. The contact says: “Ask him.”

### [阵-056] harbor_cargo · 回响3
【原文】
你翻口袋找零钱的时候，摸到一张折叠的纸。展开一看，是一行编码。
你不记得什么时候抄的。但你认得这是灰港的格式：日期，部位，来源，去向。
【新文】
Digging for change in your pocket, you find a folded piece of paper. Opened out, it is a line of code.
You do not remember copying it down. But you recognize the Ashport format: date, part, source, destination.

### [阵-057] harbor_cargo · 回响4
【原文】
你在人群中下意识地扫编号——手腕内侧、耳后、踝关节上方。
灰港的习惯。你在数这条街上有多少个零件是你经手的。
【新文】
In a crowd you find yourself scanning for numbers—inside the wrist, behind the ear, above the ankle.
An Ashport habit. You are counting how many parts on this street went through your hands.

### [阵-058] harbor_secondhand · 事件正文
【原文】
黑诊所的术后恢复室。你隔壁床的客人在装一只二手眼。
手术很成功。他睁开新眼的第一秒，愣住了。
「这间屋子，我来过。」他说。他是第一次来灰港。
医生不动声色地在病历上写了一行字：「移植后既视感，常见，观察。」

你知道医生在说谎。常见是真的，但不是「既视感」。
是前一任主人的视觉记忆残留在光感芯片的缓存里。
他看见的不是这间屋子。他看见的是上一个人最后看见的东西。
【新文】
The recovery room of a back-room clinic. The client in the next bed is being fitted with a secondhand eye.
The surgery went well. In the first second after he opens the new eye, he freezes.
“This room. I’ve been here before,” he says. It is his first time in Ashport.
Without a flicker the doctor writes a line in his notes: “Post-transplant déjà vu, common, observe.”

You know the doctor is lying. Common is true, but it is not déjà vu.
It is the previous owner’s visual memory left in the cache of the photosensor.
What he sees is not this room. What he sees is the last thing the previous person saw.

### [阵-059] harbor_secondhand · 选项1
【原文】
告诉他真相。
【新文】
Tell him the truth.

### [阵-060] harbor_secondhand · 选项1 成功
【原文】
他安静了很久。然后他用新眼睛看了一圈屋子，很慢，像在和上一个主人的目光告别。
「那我得好好用。」他说，「替他多看几样东西。」
【新文】
He is quiet for a long time. Then he looks slowly around the room with the new eye, as though seeing the previous owner’s gaze off.
“Then I’d better use it well,” he says. “See a few extra things for him.”

### [阵-061] harbor_secondhand · 选项1 失败

麻药补了三针他才安静下来。医生出门时看了你一眼，什么也没说。
【新文】
He jerks upright and starts clawing at his own eye socket. You and the doctor hold him down, one side each.
It takes three more shots of anesthetic before he goes quiet. On the way out the doctor gives you a look and says nothing.

### [阵-062] harbor_secondhand · 选项2

【新文】

### [阵-063] harbor_secondhand · 选项2 成功
【原文】
「是吗？」他松了口气，「那就好。我还以为这只眼有问题。」
你微笑着，没告诉他你的「既视感」从没消失过——
你左手中指的屈伸节奏到现在还不是你自己的。那是一个钢琴家的手指。
你不弹琴。但你的手指记得。
【新文】
“Really?” He lets out a breath. “Good. I thought there was something wrong with the eye.”
You smile and do not tell him your own déjà vu never went away—
the flex-and-extend rhythm of the middle finger of your left hand is still not yours. It belonged to a pianist.
You do not play. But your finger remembers.

### [阵-064] harbor_secondhand · 选项2 失败

【新文】
He half believes you. The story you made up is too general; it does not sound like something you lived.
“You’re humoring me.” He smiles bitterly. “Never mind. It’s in now.”

### [阵-065] harbor_secondhand · 选项3

### [阵-066] harbor_secondhand · 选项3 成功
【原文】
医生把你拉进手术室，关上门。
「退？退给谁？」她摘下手套，露出自己的双手——左手是原装的，
右手接缝处隐约可见一排编号。「你以为我的手是哪来的？」
她把指关节一个一个屈伸给你看：「这只手以前是个裁缝的。
我现在缝合比念书时好三倍。这不是残留，这是天赋。」
她重新戴上手套：「灰港不卖零件。灰港卖手艺。」
【新文】
The doctor pulls you into the theater and shuts the door.
“Back? Back to whom?” She takes off her gloves and shows you her hands—the left is the one she was born with,
and along the seam of the right there is a faint row of numbers. “Where do you think my hand came from?”
She flexes the knuckles for you one at a time. “This hand used to belong to a tailor.
My suturing is three times better than it was in school. That isn’t residue. That is a gift.”
She puts the gloves back on. “Ashport doesn’t sell parts. Ashport sells craft.”

### [阵-067] harbor_secondhand · 选项3 失败

你没看过。没人看过。灰港的协议只有一条不成文的：装了就是你的。
【新文】
The doctor does not look up. “The returns clause is on page seven of the agreement. Read it?”
You have not. Nobody has. Ashport’s agreement has exactly one unwritten clause: once it is in, it is yours.

There is a young man in the recovery room who has just had an arm fitted. He is opening and closing the fist over and over, looking more and more confused.
“This hand… plays guitar,” he says quietly. He does not play guitar.

### [阵-069] harbor_secondhand · 回响2

In the instant you close your eyes, you see a door you have never seen.
Not a dream. The last frame from the previous owner, in the chip of your left eye.
The door is shut. You will never know what is behind it.

### [阵-070] harbor_secondhand · 技能之声·机械亲和
【原文】
【机械亲和】缓存深度取决于芯片制程。28纳米的能存三到五秒的运动序列。7纳米的……够存一首完整的曲子。
【新文】
MACHINE AFFINITY — Cache depth depends on the process node. Twenty-eight nanometer holds three to five seconds of motion sequence. Seven nanometer… holds a whole piece of music.

### [阵-071] harbor_passenger · 事件正文
【原文】
后半夜，码头仓库。一个女人抱着孩子坐在鱼箱上。
她们从纯血区来。孩子先天心脏缺损，那边的医生说能治，但要装一枚人工瓣膜。
纯血区不允许。她带孩子翻了三道检查站到灰港。
「我不在乎教义，」她的声音很平，「我在乎她能活到上学。」

诊所能做这台手术。但孩子装了瓣膜就再也回不了纯血区——
扫描门一过，心跳的频率就会出卖那枚金属。
也就是说，你帮了这个忙，这个孩子一辈子是灰港人。
【新文】
Late in the night, a dock warehouse. A woman sits on a fish crate holding a child.
They have come from the pure-blood district. The child was born with a heart defect; the doctors there said it can be treated, but it needs an artificial valve.
The pure-blood district does not allow it. She brought the child through three checkpoints to Ashport.
“I don’t care about doctrine,” she says, level. “I care that she lives long enough to go to school.”

The clinic can do the surgery. But once the child has the valve she can never go back to the pure-blood district—
one pass through a scanner gate and the frequency of her heartbeat gives the metal away.
Which is to say: if you help, this child is an Ashport child for the rest of her life.

### [阵-072] harbor_passenger · 选项1
【原文】
安排手术。
【新文】
Arrange the surgery.

### [阵-073] harbor_passenger · 选项1 成功
【原文】
手术四小时。你在门外听了四小时的心电监护仪的声音。
第三小时的时候，节奏变了——多了一个不属于肉体的频率，
细小、精确、不知疲倦。
孩子醒来的时候说胸口凉凉的。她妈妈说那是新心跳的温度，暖一暖就好了。
【新文】
Four hours. You listen to the cardiac monitor outside the door for four hours.
In the third hour the rhythm changes—there is a frequency in it now that does not belong to a body,
small, exact, tireless.
When the child wakes she says her chest feels cool. Her mother says that is the temperature of a new heartbeat, and it will warm up.

### [阵-074] harbor_passenger · 选项1 失败
【原文】
术中出了一点状况——孩子的胸腔比预期的小，标准瓣膜装不进去。
医生临时拿锉刀把瓣膜磨小了一圈。能用。但接缝处没有原来那么严丝合缝。
以后每到变天，那个接缝会发出极细的哨音。
只有孩子自己听得见。
【新文】
Something goes wrong midway—the child’s chest cavity is smaller than expected and the standard valve will not fit.
The doctor takes a file to it there and then and grinds it down a size. It works. But the seam is not as tight as it should be.
From now on, whenever the weather turns, that seam gives out a very fine whistle.
Only the child can hear it.

### [阵-075] harbor_passenger · 选项2
【原文】
找一种能骗过扫描门的瓣膜型号。
【新文】
Find a valve model that can fool a scanner gate.

### [阵-076] harbor_passenger · 选项2 成功
【原文】
灰港地下有一种叫「鬼瓣」的特殊型号：生物陶瓷外壳，声纳回波和真肉几乎一样。
贵三倍，但扫描门过得去。
你帮她联系了供货商。供货商要求先验孩子的胸片——
不是为了医学，是为了确认尺码。跟量鞋一样。

鬼瓣装好了。孩子可以回纯血区。她会带着一颗假装是真的心长大。
灰港的生意，有时候就是帮人假装。
【新文】
Underground in Ashport there is a special model they call the ghost valve: bioceramic shell, sonar return almost identical to real flesh.
Three times the price, but it gets through the gates.
You put her in touch with a supplier. The supplier wants the child’s chest films first—
not for medicine. To confirm the size. The way you measure for shoes.

The ghost valve goes in. The child can go back to the pure-blood district. She will grow up with a heart that pretends to be real.
Ashport’s business is sometimes just helping people pretend.

### [阵-077] harbor_passenger · 选项2 失败

最后还是装的标准型。回不去了。
女人没有哭。她开始问灰港的学区怎么划。
【新文】
Even the smallest ghost valve is a size too large. The child’s chest cannot take it.
In the end it is the standard model. There is no going back.
The woman does not cry. She starts asking how Ashport’s school districts are drawn.

### [阵-078] harbor_passenger · 选项3
【原文】
劝她回去。「灰港不适合养孩子。」

### [阵-079] harbor_passenger · 选项3 结果
【原文】
「灰港不适合养孩子。」你说。
她看着你，眼神平静得可怕：「纯血区也不适合养一个心脏有洞的孩子。」
你闭上了嘴。她抱着孩子坐在鱼箱上，等天亮，等下一个愿意帮忙的人。

你走到仓库门口的时候停了一步。没有回头。但你把诊所的地址写在一张鱼票背面，
压在她脚边的箱子上。
【新文】
“Ashport is no place to raise a child,” you say.
She looks at you, and her eyes are frighteningly calm: “The pure-blood district is no place to raise a child with a hole in her heart.”
You shut your mouth. She sits on the fish crate holding the child, waiting for daylight, waiting for the next person willing to help.

At the warehouse door you stop for a step. You do not look back. But you write the clinic’s address on the back of a fish ticket
and weight it down on the crate beside her foot.

### [阵-080] harbor_passenger · 回响1
【原文】
码头边一个小女孩在跳房子。她跳得很高，落地很轻，
胸腔里隐约传来一声极细的金属哨音。变天了。
【新文】
A small girl is playing hopscotch at the edge of the dock. She jumps high and lands light,
and from inside her chest comes a very fine metal whistle. The weather is turning.

### [阵-081] harbor_passenger · 回响2
【原文】
码头小学放学了。一群孩子跑过你身边。你注意到至少三个孩子跑步的姿势不太对——
他们在保护胸口。灰港的孩子，有些是带着秘密长大的。
【新文】
School is out at the dock primary. A crowd of children runs past you. You notice at least three of them run oddly—
they are protecting their chests. Some of Ashport’s children grow up carrying a secret.

### [阵-082] harbor_fog_night · 事件正文
【原文】
灰港要搬家了。
有人举报了诊所的位置。三天后联合执法队来拆。
三天不够搬一间诊所——但够搬最重要的东西：病历。
十七年的病历，每一份都是一条罪证，每一份也是一个人活下来的收据。

今夜大雾。码头能见度不足五米。这是搬家的最好天气，也是最坏天气——
你有可能把箱子搬进海里。
【新文】
Ashport is moving.
Somebody reported the clinic’s location. In three days a joint enforcement team comes to take it apart.
Three days is not enough to move a clinic—but it is enough to move the thing that matters: the case notes.
Seventeen years of them. Every one is a piece of evidence of a crime, and every one is somebody’s receipt for staying alive.

Tonight there is heavy fog. Visibility on the docks is under five meters. This is the best weather for a move, and the worst—
you could quite easily carry a crate straight into the sea.

### [阵-083] harbor_fog_night · 选项1
【原文】
组织人手，分批搬运。
【新文】
Get people together and move it in loads.

### [阵-084] harbor_fog_night · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你在雾里吹了三声哨——灰港的老暗号，意思是「集合，不问为什么」。
来了十二个人。最小的十五岁，胸口有哨音的那种；最老的七十，
两条腿都是灰港诊所装的。
他们排成一列，每人间隔两步，靠声音传递方向。
四小时搬完。最后一箱递到新址的时候，雾散了。
港务局的人看见十三个人站在空码头上，什么也没有。
【新文】
You whistle three times into the fog—Ashport’s old signal, meaning gather, don’t ask why.
Twelve people come. The youngest is fifteen, one of the ones with a whistle in the chest; the oldest is seventy,
both legs fitted at an Ashport clinic.
They form a line two paces apart and pass direction along by voice.
Four hours to move it all. As the last crate goes into the new address, the fog lifts.
The port authority sees thirteen people standing on an empty dock with nothing there at all.

### [阵-085] harbor_fog_night · 选项1 失败
【原文】
第三趟的时候，有人滑进了海里。箱子沉了，人捞上来了。
沉的那箱是十一年前到八年前的病历。三年的人，就这么没了名字。
你不知道那三年里有没有你认识的人。你不敢查。
【新文】
On the third trip somebody slides into the water. The crate goes down; the person comes up.
The crate that sank was the notes from eleven years ago to eight years ago. Three years of people, and just like that they have no names.
You do not know whether anyone you knew was in those three years. You do not dare check.

### [阵-086] harbor_fog_night · 选项2
【原文】
不搬了，全部烧掉。「灰港不需要历史，灰港需要活人。」
【新文】
Stop moving them. Burn the lot. “Ashport doesn’t need a history. Ashport needs living people.”

### [阵-087] harbor_fog_night · 选项2 结果
【原文】
火烧了四十分钟。纸质病历烧起来意外地快，像它们一直在等这一天。
十七年的名字、血型、过敏源、术后嘱咐，变成灰，被海风吹向城区。
有人站在旁边哭。你没哭。你在数：十七年，大概四千个人。
四千个人今晚失去了自己活下来的证据。

但灰港还在。灰港从来不是一间诊所。灰港是一个动词。
【新文】
The fire burns for forty minutes. Paper notes go up surprisingly fast, as though they have been waiting for this.
Seventeen years of names, blood types, allergies, post-op instructions turn to ash and blow inland on the sea wind.
Somebody stands beside it crying. You do not. You are counting: seventeen years, roughly four thousand people.
Four thousand people lost the evidence that they survived tonight.

But Ashport is still here. Ashport was never a clinic. Ashport is a verb.

### [阵-088] harbor_fog_night · 选项3
【原文】
把病历数字化，传进加密网络。
【新文】
Digitize the notes and push them onto an encrypted network.

### [阵-089] harbor_fog_night · 选项3 成功
【原文】
你用了整夜。扫描仪的灯在雾里一亮一灭，像灯塔。
最后一份病历扫完时，天已经亮了。加密包上传到三个匿名节点。
从今天起，灰港的记忆住在电磁波里——摸不着，删不掉，
和那些二手义肢里残留的肌肉记忆一样，藏在看不见的地方继续活着。
【新文】
It takes you all night. The scanner lamp blinks on and off in the fog like a lighthouse.
When the last file is scanned it is already light. The encrypted package goes up to three anonymous nodes.
From today Ashport’s memory lives in electromagnetic waves—untouchable, undeletable,
like the muscle memory left in secondhand prosthetics, going on living where nobody can see it.

### [阵-090] harbor_fog_night · 选项3 失败
【原文】
扫描到一半停电了。充电宝只够扫前七年。后十年还是纸。
你做了一个决定：把后十年藏进二手义肢的存储芯片里——
每副义肢塞一点，分散在整个灰港的客户身上。
从此，每一个从灰港走出去的人，身体里都多了几个别人的名字。
【新文】
The power goes halfway through the scanning. The battery pack only covers the first seven years. The last ten stay on paper.
So you make a decision: hide the last ten inside the storage chips of secondhand prosthetics—
a little in each, spread across every client Ashport has.
From then on, everyone who walks out of Ashport carries a few other people’s names inside their body.

### [阵-091] harbor_fog_night · 回响1
【原文】
码头上有一块烧焦的地面，长出了草。没人记得这里烧过什么，但草长得比别处绿。
【新文】
There is a burned patch of ground on the dock with grass growing on it. Nobody remembers what was burned here, but the grass comes up greener than elsewhere.

### [阵-092] harbor_fog_night · 回响2
【原文】
你弯腰的时候，左膝盖的义体发出一声短促的嗡鸣——不是故障，
是里面存着的某个人的术后嘱咐在定时提醒：「每六小时翻一次身。」

【新文】
As you bend down, the prosthetic in your left knee gives one short hum—not a fault.

A medical order for a person you never met, living in your joint.

### [阵-093] harbor_ledger · 事件正文
【原文】
老码头长叫你去他的集装箱办公室。
他把一本发黄的账簿推到你面前。不是钱的账——是零件的账。
每一行：日期，部位，来源编码，去向编码。
「灰港的规矩，零件过手名字落水。但我留了编码。」他指着某一行，
「你看这个。左眼，来源K-4412，去向D-0078。」
他翻到下一页：「两年后，右手，来源D-0078，去向M-2291。」
再翻：「又两年，脊柱节段，来源M-2291，去向K-4412。」

K-4412的左眼给了D-0078。D-0078的右手给了M-2291。M-2291的脊柱给了K-4412。
三个人。每人身上都有另外两个人的零件。一条首尾相接的环。

码头长合上账簿：「灰港干了十七年，这种环我见过不下二十个。
时间够长的话，所有人身上都会有所有人的零件。」
他看着你：「你猜，到那一天，谁是谁？」
【新文】
The old harbormaster calls you to his shipping-container office.
He pushes a yellowed ledger across to you. Not a ledger of money—a ledger of parts.
Every line: date, part, source code, destination code.
“Ashport’s rule is that parts pass through hands and names go in the water. But I kept the codes.” He points at a line.
“Look at this one. Left eye, source K-4412, destination D-0078.”
He turns the page. “Two years later, right hand, source D-0078, destination M-2291.”
And again: “Two years after that, spinal segment, source M-2291, destination K-4412.”

K-4412’s left eye went to D-0078. D-0078’s right hand went to M-2291. M-2291’s spine went to K-4412.
Three people. Each one carrying parts of the other two. A ring, closed end to end.

The harbormaster shuts the ledger. “Ashport has run seventeen years. I have seen no fewer than twenty rings like that.
Given long enough, everybody will be carrying everybody’s parts.”
He looks at you. “On that day—who do you suppose is who?”

### [阵-094] harbor_ledger · 选项1
【原文】
「那就没有谁是谁了。」
【新文】
“Then nobody is anybody.”

### [阵-095] harbor_ledger · 选项1 成功
【原文】
码头长慢慢点了根烟。「你知道灰港为什么叫灰港吗？」
你摇头。
「不是因为雾。是因为零件到了这里，黑的白的都变成灰的。
来源不重要了，去向不重要了。重要的是它还能用。」
他把账簿推进抽屉：「灰色不是没有颜色。灰色是所有颜色混在一起。」
【新文】
The harbormaster takes his time lighting a cigarette. “You know why Ashport is called Ashport?”
You shake your head.
“Not the fog. Because parts that arrive here, the black ones and the white ones, all come out the color of ash.
Where it came from stops mattering. Where it is going stops mattering. What matters is that it still works.”
He pushes the ledger into a drawer. “Ash is not the absence of color. Ash is every color mixed together.”

### [阵-096] harbor_ledger · 选项1 失败
【原文】
码头长摇了摇头。「太轻巧了。」他说，「你再想想。」
你没想出来。但你记住了那个环。三个人的零件首尾相接，
像一条咬着自己尾巴的蛇。
【新文】
The harbormaster shakes his head. “Too neat,” he says. “Think again.”
You do not get there. But you remember that ring. Three people’s parts joined end to end,
like a snake with its own tail in its mouth.

### [阵-097] harbor_ledger · 选项2

「谁是谁不重要。重要的是谁在用。」
【新文】
“Who is who doesn’t matter. What matters is who is using it.”

### [阵-098] harbor_ledger · 选项2 成功
【原文】
码头长愣了一下，然后笑了。「来灰港的人都带着自己的名字和别人的零件。
你是第一个说出这句话的。」
他把账簿递给你：「那从今天起，这本账你来记。
码头长不是一个人，码头长是一支笔。上一个码头长的手，现在在你认识的某个人身上。」
【新文】
The harbormaster blinks, then laughs. “Everyone who comes to Ashport brings their own name and other people’s parts.
You are the first to say it out loud.”
He hands you the ledger. “Then from today you keep it.
The harbormaster is not a person. The harbormaster is a pen. The last harbormaster’s hand is on somebody you know.”

### [阵-099] harbor_ledger · 选项2 失败
【原文】
码头长叹了口气。「你说的对。但『在用』也是一个临时状态。」
他敲了敲自己的胸口，金属回声：「总有一天你也会变成来源编码。」
【新文】
The harbormaster sighs. “You’re right. But being used is a temporary state too.”
He knocks on his own chest; the echo is metal. “One day you become a source code as well.”

### [阵-100] harbor_ledger · 选项3

问他：「我身上有没有别人的？」
【新文】
Ask him: “Is there anybody else’s in me?”

### [阵-101] harbor_ledger · 选项3 结果
【原文】
码头长翻到账簿最后几页。手指划过编码，停在一行上。
他看着那行字看了很久。然后他合上了账簿。
「有，」他说，「但我不打算告诉你是哪个零件。」
他站起来走到门口：「有些事知道了你会开始区分。
这块是我的，那块不是。但你身上没有一块零件是单独活着的。」
【新文】
The harbormaster turns to the last pages of the ledger. His finger runs down the codes and stops on a line.
He looks at that line for a long time. Then he closes the ledger.
“There is,” he says, “but I am not going to tell you which part.”
He gets up and goes to the door. “Some things, once you know them, you start dividing.
This piece is mine, that piece isn’t. But there is not one part of you that is alive on its own.”

### [阵-102] harbor_ledger · 技能之声·街智

【街智】「K-4412。」你突然说出一个编号。你不知道自己怎么知道的。码头长抬头看你，眼神变了。
【新文】
STREETWISE — “K-4412,” you say suddenly. You do not know how you know it. The harbormaster looks up, and something in his eyes has changed.

### [阵-103] finale_harbor · 事件正文
【原文】
【终幕】灰港的最后一个夜晚。
不是被查封——是海平面。灰港建在旧城区最低处，年年涨潮年年退。
今年潮水不退了。地下诊所的手术台已经泡在半米深的海水里。

码头长说：走。带上能带的。
你站在齐膝的水里，面前是那只军绿色防水箱。
箱子里不是义肢——是账簿。十七年的编码，谁的零件去了谁的身上。

潮水还在涨。你只能带走一样东西。
【新文】
FINALE — Ashport’s last night.
Not a raid—sea level. Ashport was built on the lowest ground in the old city; the tide comes up every year and goes back down every year.
This year it is not going back down. The operating table in the underground clinic is already standing in half a meter of seawater.

The harbormaster says: go. Take what you can carry.
You stand knee-deep in water with the army-green waterproof crate in front of you.
What is in the crate is not prosthetics—it is the ledger. Seventeen years of codes: whose parts went into whom.

The tide is still rising. You can take one thing.

### [阵-104] finale_harbor · 选项1
【原文】
带账簿。灰港可以没有码头，不能没有记忆。
【新文】
Take the ledger. Ashport can do without a dock. It cannot do without a memory.

### [阵-105] finale_harbor · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你把箱子顶在头上，水漫到胸口。出了码头的时候天已经亮了。
你浑身湿透，站在高地上回头看——灰港沉在水面以下，
只有诊所的红十字灯还在水下亮着，像一颗不肯闭上的眼。

你打开箱子检查。账簿没湿。十七年的编码完好无损。
灰港沉了。但每个人身上的每个零件，都还记得自己从哪里来。
【新文】
You put the crate on your head; the water comes up to your chest. It is light by the time you are out of the docks.
Soaked through, you stand on high ground and look back—Ashport is under the surface,
and only the clinic’s red cross is still lit down there, like an eye that will not close.

You open the crate and check. The ledger is dry. Seventeen years of codes, intact.
Ashport has gone under. But every part in every body still remembers where it came from.

### [阵-106] finale_harbor · 选项1 失败
【原文】
水太深了。箱子太重了。你在第三个拐弯的时候脚下一滑，
箱子脱手。你伸手去捞——够到了锁扣，但锁扣断了。
账簿散落在水里。纸页吸水膨胀，编码变成一团灰色的糊。

灰色。所有颜色混在一起。
你空着手走出码头。十七年的来源和去向，从此真的落了水。
【新文】
The water is too deep. The crate is too heavy. At the third corner your foot goes out from under you
and the crate is gone from your hands. You reach for it—you get the catch, and the catch breaks.
The ledger scatters into the water. The pages swell and the codes go to a grey paste.

Grey. Every color mixed together.
You walk out of the docks empty-handed. Seventeen years of sources and destinations really did go in the water.

### [阵-107] finale_harbor · 选项2

【新文】
Take the last of the prosthetics. People matter more than records.

### [阵-108] finale_harbor · 选项2 成功
【原文】
你把六副义肢分别绑在身上——两条胳膊各挂一副，背上背两副，
腰上系两副。你看起来像一棵挂满了零件的树。
水退了以后，你在高地上一副一副解下来。
有三个人在等。他们等了一夜。
你把义肢递给他们的时候，手上沾着海水和机油。
一个人低声说：「灰港没了，你还在就行。」
【新文】
You strap six prosthetics to yourself—one hanging from each arm, two on your back,
two tied at the waist. You look like a tree hung with parts.
When the water goes down you take them off one at a time up on the high ground.
Three people are waiting. They waited all night.
As you hand the prosthetics over there is seawater and engine oil on your hands.
One of them says quietly: “Ashport’s gone. As long as you’re still here.”

### [阵-109] finale_harbor · 选项2 失败
【原文】
义肢太重了，水流太急。你只带出来两副。
第三副在出口处被暗流卷走，撞在堤坝上，散了架。
那只右手的食指还保持着握笔的弧度，单独漂在水面上，
像在给谁写最后一个字。
【新文】
The prosthetics are too heavy and the current is too fast. You get two of them out.
The third is taken by an undertow at the exit, thrown against the seawall, and comes apart.
The index finger of that right hand is still holding the shape of a grip on a pen,
floating alone on the surface, as though writing somebody one last word.

### [阵-110] finale_harbor · 选项3

Take nothing. Be the last to leave, and lock the door from the inside.

### [阵-111] finale_harbor · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你把门从里面锁上。然后从窗户翻出去。
水面合拢。灰港沉下去的过程很安静，没有气泡，没有回声。
像一个人闭上眼——不是死了，是决定不再看。

你站在高地上淋雨，口袋里只有一把诊所的钥匙。
钥匙打不开任何门了。但你没有扔。

灰港不是一间诊所。灰港不是一座码头。
灰港是一把湿透了的钥匙，装在某个还活着的人口袋里。

只要还有人需要一个不问来路的地方，灰港就会在别处重新长出来。
下一个灰港也许不在海边，也许天色很好，也许连「港」字都不占。
**但所有需要它的人都会认得出它。** 名字是跟着需要走的，
不是跟着地图走的。
【新文】
You lock the door from the inside. Then you climb out through the window.
The water closes over. Ashport goes under quietly, with no bubbles and no echo.
Like a person closing their eyes—not dead. Only deciding not to look anymore.

You stand in the rain on high ground with nothing in your pocket but a key to the clinic.
The key will not open any door now. You do not throw it away.

Ashport is not a clinic. Ashport is not a dock.
Ashport is a soaked key in the pocket of somebody still alive.

As long as there are people who need a place that does not ask where you came from, Ashport will grow back somewhere else.
The next Ashport may not be by the sea, the weather there may be fine, it may not even have a port in the name.
**But everybody who needs it will know it when they see it.** Names follow need,
not maps.

### [阵-112] temple_scripture · 事件正文
【原文】
圣殿地下室的经文抄写课。圣殿派保存着一块刻了字的金属薄片——
据说是上一个纪元遗留的唯一信物。所有信徒每年都要手抄一遍全文。
问题是：薄片上有一个词被腐蚀了，只剩偏旁。
两百年来，圣殿派一直把它抄成「归」——魂归苍天。
今晚你拿着放大镜看了很久。腐蚀的痕迹下面，残存的笔画不像「归」。
更像「跻」。跻身苍天。

「归」是回家。「跻」是攀登。一字之差，教义从「保持完整等待回收」
变成「不断改造直到够格」。
【新文】
Scripture-copying class in the Temple basement. The Temple keeps one thin sheet of metal with writing cut into it—
said to be the only token left from the previous epoch. Every believer copies the whole text out by hand once a year.
The trouble is that one word on the sheet has corroded away, and only part of it is left.
For two hundred years the Temple has copied it as RECEIVED—the soul is received into Heaven.
Tonight you spent a long time on it with a magnifying glass. Under the corrosion, the surviving strokes do not look like RECEIVED.
They look more like ACHIEVED. Heaven achieved.

RECEIVED is being taken home. ACHIEVED is climbing. One word apart, and the doctrine turns from
stay whole and wait to be collected into keep remaking yourself until you qualify.

### [阵-113] temple_scripture · 选项1
【原文】
照旧抄「归」。教义经不起第二种读法。
【新文】
Copy RECEIVED as always. The doctrine cannot survive a second reading.

### [阵-114] temple_scripture · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你抄完整篇经文，手腕酸了三天。最后一笔落下的时候，你在想：
两百年，每年一遍，多少双手在这个字上停留过？多少人看到了「跻」，抄下了「归」？
也许你不是第一个。也许这间地下室的历史，就是一部反复选择「归」的历史。
【新文】
You finish the whole text; your wrist aches for three days. As the last stroke goes down, you are thinking:
two hundred years, once a year—how many hands have paused on that word? How many of them saw ACHIEVED and wrote RECEIVED?
Perhaps you are not the first. Perhaps the history of this basement is a history of choosing RECEIVED, over and over.

### [阵-115] temple_scripture · 选项1 失败
【原文】
你的手在「归」字上抖了一下。墨迹洇开，像一扇小窗。
你赶紧用新纸覆盖上去重抄。但你知道那一笔抖动的真正原因。
【新文】
Your hand shakes on the word. The ink spreads, like a small window.
You cover it quickly with fresh paper and start again. But you know the real reason your hand shook.

### [阵-116] temple_scripture · 选项2

【新文】

### [阵-117] temple_scripture · 选项2 成功
【原文】
长老拿着放大镜看了很久。久到你以为他会叫来全体信众。
他把放大镜收起来，把那块金属薄片锁回保险箱。
「孩子，」他的声音很平，「你以为我没看过吗？」
他没说它到底是「归」还是「跻」。他只说了一句：
「教义是船。船板可以换，航向不能换。」

你走出地下室的时候，听见背后落锁的声音。
你不知道他锁住的是经文，还是那个字。
【新文】
The elder studies it under the glass for a long time. Long enough that you think he is going to call the whole congregation in.
He puts the glass away and locks the metal sheet back in the safe.
“Child,” he says, and his voice is level, “do you think I have never looked?”
He does not say which word it is. He says only one thing:
“Doctrine is a ship. You may change the planks. You may not change the heading.”

On your way out of the basement you hear the lock go home behind you.
You do not know whether what he locked away was the scripture, or the word.

### [阵-118] temple_scripture · 选项2 失败
【原文】
长老听完，沉默了很久。然后他把你的抄本收走了。
「年轻人眼花是正常的。以后抄经不必用放大镜。」
他的语气没有责备。但之后一个月，地下室的灯被换成了更暗的。
【新文】
The elder hears you out and says nothing for a long time. Then he takes your copy away.
“Young eyes play tricks. There is no need to use a glass when you copy from now on.”
There is no reproach in his tone. But for the next month the basement lights are changed for dimmer ones.

### [阵-119] temple_scripture · 选项3

### [阵-120] temple_scripture · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你的抄本夹在四百份「归」中间，像一颗反转的螺丝。
没有人会翻你的抄本。抄经课的全部意义在于抄写这个动作本身——
就像打坐不是为了结论，是为了过程。
但你知道，从今天起，圣殿的四百零一份经文里，有一份在说不同的话。
一份就够了。
【新文】
Your transcript sits among four hundred copies of RECEIVED, like one screw threaded backward.
Nobody reads your transcript. The entire point of the copying class is the act of copying—
the way sitting in meditation is not for the conclusion but for the process.
But you know that from today, one of the Temple’s four hundred and one scriptures says something different.
One is enough.

### [阵-121] temple_scripture · 回响1

沉默不代表同意。沉默有时候代表还没想好怎么开口。
【新文】
At evening prayer you look at the floor covered in kneeling people and think:
how many of them have wholeness in their mouths and a child who cannot afford a valve in their minds?
Silence is not agreement. Sometimes silence means not having worked out how to start.

### [阵-122] temple_scripture · 回响2

但她握笔的力道变了。

In the copying class the person beside you stops writing. She stares at the word for a long time, and then—writes RECEIVED.

### [阵-124] temple_knees · 事件正文
【原文】
传教士死了。
葬礼按最高规格办——全血肉安葬，不动一颗螺丝。这是圣殿派对自己人
最后的敬意：你怎么来的，就怎么走。

入殓的时候出了事。

负责净身的执事掀开裤腿，停住了。
钛合金膝盖。两个。手术痕迹至少二十年。
关节处的磨损模式和他爬楼梯的步频完全吻合——
他用这双假膝盖爬了二十年楼梯，传了二十年纯血的道。

执事回头看向长老。长老合上了眼。
满屋子的人都在等一个判决。
【新文】
The preacher is dead.
The funeral is held to the highest specification—buried wholly in flesh, not one screw disturbed. This is the Temple’s last
respect to its own: you leave the way you came.

Something happens during the laying out.

The deacon washing the body lifts the trouser leg, and stops.
Titanium knees. Both of them. Surgical scarring at least twenty years old.
The wear pattern at the joints matches the cadence of his stair-climbing exactly—
twenty years of stairs on those false knees, twenty years of preaching pure blood.

The deacon looks back at the elder. The elder closes his eyes.
The whole room is waiting for a verdict.

### [阵-125] temple_knees · 选项1
【原文】
「按不完整者规格安葬。誓约没有例外。」
【新文】
“Bury him to the specification for the incomplete. The covenant makes no exceptions.”

### [阵-126] temple_knees · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你说得很硬。有几个人低下了头。
葬礼改在后半夜，没有经文，没有蜡烛。
他被埋进没有名字的那片土。坟上没有标记。
但你注意到，之后一年，每天清晨都有人来那片无名土上放一支白蜡烛。
没有人承认是自己放的。
【新文】
You say it hard. A few heads go down.
The funeral is moved to the small hours: no scripture, no candles.
He is buried in the ground that has no names. There is no marker on the grave.
But you notice that for the next year, every morning at dawn somebody leaves a white candle on that nameless ground.
Nobody admits to it.

### [阵-127] temple_knees · 选项1 失败
【原文】
你话还没说完，角落里一个老信众站起来：
「他四十层楼，爬了二十年。你爬过几层？」
你闭上了嘴。规格最终由长老定：折中，半礼。
半礼是什么意思，谁也说不清。
【新文】
Before you have finished, an old believer stands up in the corner:
“Forty floors. Twenty years. How many flights have you climbed?”
You shut your mouth. The elder settles the specification in the end: split the difference, half rites.
What half rites means, nobody can say.

### [阵-128] temple_knees · 选项2
【原文】
「把膝盖取出来，然后按全血肉安葬。死后的他是完整的。」
【新文】
“Take the knees out, then bury him wholly in flesh. In death he is whole.”

### [阵-129] temple_knees · 选项2 成功
【原文】
手术用了三小时。你在门外听着金属脱离骨骼的声音——
二十年的组织已经和钛合金长在了一起，分离的时候带出了一层骨膜。

两块膝盖被放在一个木盒里。遗体的裤腿空了一截，但形式上完整了。
长老主持了全规格葬礼。没有人提那个木盒后来去了哪里。

三天后你在圣殿的供桌上看到了它。放在最高的经卷旁边。
没有标签，没有说明。但谁都知道那是什么。
【新文】
The surgery takes three hours. Outside the door you listen to metal coming away from bone—
twenty years of tissue had grown into the titanium, and separating them brings a layer of periosteum with it.

The two knees are laid in a wooden box. The trouser legs of the body are short of a stretch now, but the form is whole.
The elder leads a full-specification funeral. Nobody mentions where the box went afterward.

Three days later you see it on the Temple’s altar. Set beside the highest scripture.
No label, no explanation. But everyone knows what it is.

### [阵-130] temple_knees · 选项2 失败
【原文】
执事拒绝动手：「我不碰死人身上的铁。」
你找了三个人，没人愿意。最后是灰港来的一个外科医生做的——
消息传开后，圣殿派在自己的葬礼上请了黑诊所的人，成了全城的笑话。
【新文】
The deacon refuses to do it: “I don’t touch iron on a dead man.”
You find three others; none will. In the end it is a surgeon out of Ashport who does it—
and when word gets out, the Temple hiring a back-room clinic for its own funeral becomes the joke of the city.

### [阵-131] temple_knees · 选项3

### [阵-132] temple_knees · 选项3 成功
【原文】
编号被锉掉了，但磨损模式骗不了人。你找到了灰港的一个旧接头人。
他翻了翻记忆：「二十年前……有个传教士找过我们。
他说他需要爬楼梯。他说如果他不爬楼梯，就没有人替他爬。」
接头人叹了口气：「我见过很多来灰港的圣殿人。他们来的时候都说是最后一次。
他是唯一一个真的只来了一次的。」
【新文】
The serial numbers have been filed off, but a wear pattern cannot lie. You find an old contact from Ashport.
He turns it over in his memory: “Twenty years ago… there was a preacher who came to us.
He said he needed to climb stairs. He said if he did not climb them, nobody would climb them for him.”
The contact sighs. “I’ve seen plenty of Temple people come to Ashport. Every one of them says it is the last time.
He is the only one who really did come only once.”

### [阵-133] temple_knees · 选项3 失败

编号被锉得太干净了。线索断在灰港和圣殿之间的那段雾里。
但你知道了一件事：传教士自己锉的。他比任何人都想消灭证据。
不是为了保护自己。是为了保护教义。
【新文】
The numbers have been filed too clean. The trail dies in the fog between Ashport and the Temple.
But you know one thing now: he filed them himself. He wanted the evidence gone more than anybody.
Not to protect himself. To protect the doctrine.

### [阵-135] temple_knees · 选项4 结果
【原文】
你走到遗体旁边，弯腰，把掀开的裤腿一层一层放回去。
布料盖住了钛合金。从外面看，他还是一个完整的人。
长老什么也没说。执事什么也没说。
裤腿下面的秘密，和他一起进了土。

葬礼按全规格办了。所有人都知道裤腿下面是什么。
所有人都假装不知道。这是圣殿派的第二种虔诚。
【新文】
You walk to the body, bend down, and fold the lifted trouser leg back, one layer at a time.
The cloth covers the titanium. From the outside, he is a whole man again.
The elder says nothing. The deacon says nothing.
What is under the trouser leg goes into the ground with him.

The funeral is held at full specification. Everyone knows what is under the trouser leg.
Everyone pretends not to. This is the Temple’s second kind of piety.

At the highest point of the Temple altar, beside the scripture, there is now an unlabeled wooden box.

【新文】
Passing the old site at Ashport, a retired contact gives you a nod.

### [阵-139] temple_trial · 事件正文
【原文】
一年一度的「血肉试炼」。圣殿派最重要的仪式：
全体信众赤脚走过一段碎石路。意义是感受疼痛——
「疼痛是血肉给你的回执，证明这具身体还归你。」

你走到第三十步的时候，发现自己不疼。

不是麻木。是你脚底那层茧——厚得像另一张皮。
码头、工地、拆解场，这些年一步一步磨出来的。
碎石硌上去，只剩一点钝钝的压力。

那层茧是纯粹的血肉。一克金属也没有。
可它让你在这条路上作了弊。
【新文】
The yearly Trial of Flesh. The Temple’s most important rite:
the whole congregation walks a stretch of gravel road barefoot. The point is to feel pain—
“Pain is the receipt the flesh gives you. Proof that this body is still yours.”

At the thirtieth step you find that it does not hurt.

Not numbness. It is the callus on your soles—thick as a second skin.
The docks, the building sites, the breaking yard: ground on a step at a time over the years.
The gravel goes in and all that is left is a dull pressure.

That callus is pure flesh. Not one gram of metal in it.
And it has made you a cheat on this road.

### [阵-140] temple_trial · 选项1
【原文】
咬牙走完。假装还在疼。
【新文】
Grit through it. Act as though it still hurts.

### [阵-141] temple_trial · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你把表情调整到疼痛应有的样子。牙关咬紧，额头冒汗——
但汗是真的。假装疼痛需要的意志力，比承受疼痛更大。

走完全程。没有人怀疑。你通过了试炼。
但你自己知道：你通过的不是血肉试炼，你通过的是谎言试炼。
【新文】
You set your face to what pain should look like. Jaw clenched, sweat on the forehead—
but the sweat is real. Faking pain takes more will than bearing it.

You walk the whole road. Nobody doubts you. You pass the trial.
But you know what you passed: not the trial of flesh. The trial of lying.

### [阵-142] temple_trial · 选项1 失败
【原文】
走到第四十步你笑了。不是苦笑，是真的觉得好笑——
你在一群真正感到疼痛的人中间假装疼痛。
旁边的人看见你笑，以为你在忍到极限。「好样的，」他说。
你笑得更厉害了。
【新文】
At the fortieth step you laugh. Not bitterly—you genuinely find it funny:
you are faking pain in the middle of a crowd of people who are actually in it.
The man beside you sees you laughing and assumes you are enduring to your limit. “Good man,” he says.
You laugh harder.

### [阵-143] temple_trial · 选项2
【原文】
停下来。当众翻开脚掌。
【新文】
Stop. Turn your soles up for everyone to see.

### [阵-144] temple_trial · 选项2 成功
【原文】
你蹲下来，脱了袜子，把脚底翻给身后的人看。
碎石路上安静了三十秒。三十秒够一段布道了。

你没有布道。你只说了一句：「这不是装的。」
然后又补了半句：「这是走出来的。」

长老走过来。他蹲下来，看了你的脚很久。
然后他脱了自己的鞋。

他脚底那层比你的还厚。厚到你一眼就看得出，
他不是这两年才不疼的。

碎石路上，两个赤脚的人对视。你们达成了一项无人见证的协议。
【新文】
You crouch, pull off your socks, and hold your soles up to the people behind you.
The gravel road goes quiet for thirty seconds. Thirty seconds is long enough for a sermon.

You do not preach. You say one thing: “This isn’t faked.”
Then you add half a sentence: “This was walked into me.”

The elder comes over. He crouches and looks at your feet a long time.
Then he takes off his own shoes.

The callus on his soles is thicker than yours. Thick enough that you can see at a glance
that it has not been two years since he stopped hurting.

On the gravel road, two barefoot people look at each other. You have reached an agreement with no witnesses.

### [阵-145] temple_trial · 选项2 失败
【原文】
你翻开脚掌，等一句话。

没有人接。有人低头看了看自己的脚，
然后把脚往袍子底下缩了一点。

主持仪式的人很客气地请你回到队伍最后面，重走一遍。
「这次慢一点，」他说，「感受一下。」

你重走了一遍。还是不疼。
【新文】
You turn your soles up and wait for someone to speak.

Nobody does. One person glances down at their own feet
and draws them a little further under their robe.

The officiant asks you very politely to go back to the end of the line and walk it again.
“Slower this time,” he says. “Feel it.”

You walk it again. It still does not hurt.

### [阵-146] temple_trial · 选项3
【原文】
继续走，但不假装。你的步态会告诉所有人你不疼了。
【新文】
Keep walking, but don’t fake it. Your gait will tell everyone you feel nothing.

### [阵-147] temple_trial · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你走完了碎石路。步态平稳，表情平静。
旁边的人拧着脸一步一哆嗦。你像在走地毯。
没有人指出来。但所有人都看到了差别。

走完之后，一个年轻信徒追上你：
「你……不疼吗？」
你想了想：「疼。但不是脚在疼。」
他没听懂。他会想很久。
【新文】
You walk the gravel road out. Steady gait, calm face.
The people beside you are screwing up their faces and flinching at every step. You might be walking on carpet.
Nobody points it out. But everybody sees the difference.

Afterward a young believer catches up with you:
“You… it doesn’t hurt?”
You think about it. “It hurts. Just not the feet.”
He does not understand. He will think about it for a long time.

### [阵-148] temple_trial · 技能之声·坚忍
【原文】
【坚忍】这层茧不是长出来的，是被磨出来的。
三年码头，两年拆解场。它比任何一件义体都更是你自己挣的。
【新文】
ENDURANCE — This callus was not grown. It was ground on.
Three years on the docks, two at the breaking yard. It is more yours than any prosthetic you could buy.

### [阵-149] temple_vault · 事件正文
【原文】
你被带进圣殿最深的房间。只有长老和三名「灯守」有钥匙。
房间中央是一个玻璃柜。柜子里不是经文。

是一只手。

金属的。五指舒展，掌心朝上，放在一块天鹅绒上面。
关节处的工艺精细到每一条指纹都有。
手腕断面打磨得光滑如镜，像是被人故意保存的——不是残骸，是标本。

长老站在柜子旁边：「建殿的时候从地基里挖出来的。
比这座城所有的历史都老。我们验过——合金成分不属于任何已知制造商。」
他看着你：「经文说，大地与星空之子以完整的血肉归天。
可这只手在告诉我们：最早的天之子，是金属做的。」
【新文】
You are taken into the deepest room in the Temple. Only the elder and three Lampkeepers hold keys.
In the middle of the room is a glass case. What is in it is not scripture.

It is a hand.

Metal. Fingers open, palm up, resting on a piece of velvet.
The work at the joints is fine enough that every fingerprint is there.
The cut face of the wrist is polished to a mirror, as if somebody preserved it deliberately—not wreckage. A specimen.

The elder stands beside the case: “Dug out of the foundations when the Temple was built.
Older than all the history of this city. We have had it tested—the alloy does not belong to any known manufacturer.”
He looks at you: “The scripture says a child of Earth and starry Heaven goes to Heaven whole, in flesh.
But this hand is telling us that the earliest child of Heaven was made of metal.”

### [阵-150] temple_vault · 选项1
【原文】
「这就是你们把经文锁在地下室的原因。」
【新文】
“That is why you keep the scripture locked in a basement.”

### [阵-151] temple_vault · 选项1 成功
【原文】
「你是第四个猜到的。」长老从袖子里取出一份名单——
四个名字，跨越一百多年。「每一代灯守里，总有一个人到这个房间后问同一句话。」

他指着名单第一行：「第一个是建殿者本人。他挖出这只手的那天晚上，
写下了全部经文。『归天』那个字，是他自己选的。」
他顿了一下：「不是因为看不见真相。是因为他决定，
这座城还没准备好听真话。」

你看着那只金属手。它的掌心朝上，像在等一个握手。
【新文】
“You are the fourth to work it out.” The elder produces a list from his sleeve—
four names, spanning more than a hundred years. “In every generation of Lampkeepers there is one who comes into this room and asks that same question.”

He points to the first line: “The first was the founder himself. The night he dug this hand out,
he wrote the whole of the scripture. That word—the one about going to Heaven—he chose himself.”
He pauses. “Not because he could not see the truth. Because he decided
this city was not ready to hear it.”

You look at the metal hand. The palm is up, as though it is waiting for a handshake.

### [阵-152] temple_vault · 选项1 失败
【原文】
长老摇了摇头：「不全是。」他没解释「全」的部分是什么。
你走出密室的时候，门在身后关上。你听到了三道锁的声音。
【新文】
The elder shakes his head. “Not entirely.” He does not explain which part entirely covers.
As you leave the vault, the door closes behind you. You hear three locks.

### [阵-153] temple_vault · 选项2
【原文】
「那你们为什么还在传肉身神圣？」
【新文】

### [阵-154] temple_vault · 选项2 成功
【原文】
长老站在那只手旁边，沉默了很久。
「因为这座城里，最先被改造的永远是最穷的人。」
他的声音忽然苍老了十岁。「工伤装液压肩，欠债换一颗便宜的肾。
改造从来不是选择。改造是账单。」
他摸了摸玻璃柜：「我们传肉身神圣——不是因为肉身真的神圣。
是因为如果我们不传，穷人连说『我不想换』的理由都没有了。」

你想反驳。但你想起了码头工的液压肩，想起了那张没有「为什么」一栏的税率表。
【新文】
The elder stands beside the hand and says nothing for a long time.
“Because in this city, the first people to be augmented are always the poorest.”
His voice ages ten years all at once. “A hydraulic shoulder for a work injury, a cheap kidney against a debt.
Augmentation was never a choice. Augmentation is a bill.”
He touches the glass. “We preach that the flesh is holy—not because the flesh really is holy.
Because if we stopped, the poor would not even have a reason left to say I don’t want to trade it in.”

You want to argue. Then you remember the dockworker’s hydraulic shoulder, and a rate table with no column for why.

### [阵-155] temple_vault · 选项2 失败
【原文】
长老看了你一眼。那种看法，你在灰港的接头人脸上也见过——
「你太年轻了，还在以为问题有答案。」
他没有回答你的问题。你走出密室，带着一个比进来时更重的沉默。
【新文】
The elder gives you a look. You have seen that look before on a contact in Ashport—
you are too young, you still think questions have answers.
He does not answer yours. You leave the vault carrying a heavier silence than you brought in.

### [阵-156] temple_vault · 选项3
【原文】
伸出自己的手，和玻璃柜里的手比一比。
【新文】
Hold your own hand up against the one in the case.

### [阵-157] temple_vault · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你把自己的手掌贴在玻璃上。
玻璃另一边，那只金属手比你的大一号。五指的张开角度和人类完全一致。
你盯着自己的手——皮肤、血管、骨节——再看那只手——合金、关节、指纹。

长老走到你旁边，也把手贴上去。
玻璃上三只手。一只血肉。一只金属。一只……
你低头看长老的手——右手小指缺了一截，断面被皮肤覆盖住了，
但形状不对。不是缺了一截。是换了一截。

你和长老对视。谁都没有说话。
这间房里，三只手，没有一只是完全的。
【新文】
You lay your palm flat on the glass.
On the other side, the metal hand is a size larger than yours. The spread of the fingers is exactly human.
You stare at your own hand—skin, veins, knuckles—then at that one—alloy, joints, fingerprints.

The elder comes over and puts his hand up as well.
Three hands on the glass. One flesh. One metal. One…
You look down at the elder’s hand—the little finger of the right hand is short a section, and the end is covered with skin,
but the shape is wrong. It is not missing a section. A section has been replaced.

You and the elder look at each other. Neither of you says anything.
In this room there are three hands, and not one of them is whole.

### [阵-158] temple_vault · 回响1

你走进一间博物馆，在「史前文物」展柜里看见了一截金属指骨。

【新文】
You walk into a museum and see a metal finger bone in the prehistoric artifacts case.
The label reads: date unknown, material unknown, presumed ceremonial. You know it was not ceremonial.

### [阵-159] temple_vault · 回响2
【原文】
你在人群中看见一个戴手套的老人。没人觉得异常——这座城的冬天确实冷。
但你知道有些手套不是为了保暖，是为了保密。
【新文】
In a crowd you see an old man wearing gloves. Nobody thinks anything of it—winter in this city really is cold.
But you know that some gloves are not for warmth. They are for secrecy.

### [阵-160] temple_vault · 技能之声·逻辑
【原文】
【逻辑】这只手的合金配比不在任何工业数据库里。要么极老，要么极新——老到在这座城之前，或新到在这座城之后。
【新文】
LOGIC — The alloy ratio in that hand is in no industrial database. Either extremely old or extremely new—old enough to predate this city, or new enough to outlast it.

### [阵-161] temple_schism · 事件正文
【原文】
传教士的膝盖成了一道分水岭。
圣殿派分成了两桌人。一桌说：「他是叛徒。」一桌说：「他是圣人。」
没有第三桌——因为第三种意见需要承认膝盖和教义可以共存，
而那张桌子坐下去就等于自己是第二个传教士。

今晚两桌人要投票：传教士的名字，留在圣殿的名录上，还是擦掉。
长老坐在两桌之间，投票前只说了一句话：
「这个投票的结果，会比教义活得更久。」
【新文】
The preacher’s knees have become a watershed.
The Temple has split into two tables. One says: he was a traitor. One says: he was a saint.
There is no third table—because a third position would mean admitting that knees and doctrine can coexist,
and sitting down at that table is the same as declaring yourself the second preacher.

Tonight the two tables vote: does the preacher’s name stay on the Temple roll, or is it struck out?
The elder sits between the two tables, and before the vote says only one thing:
“The result of this vote will outlive the doctrine.”

### [阵-162] temple_schism · 选项1
【原文】
投「留」。理由：「他的膝盖是假的，路是真的。」
【新文】
Vote to keep. Because: “His knees were false. The road was real.”

### [阵-163] temple_schism · 选项1 成功
【原文】
「路是真的」这四个字在两桌之间弹了三个来回。
最后四十二票留，三十八票擦。他的名字留在了名录上。
名字旁边加了一个小小的星号。星号没有注释。
一百年后，如果还有人翻这本名录，他们会对那个星号困惑。
困惑就对了。
【新文】
The road was real goes back and forth between the two tables three times.
In the end it is forty-two to keep, thirty-eight to strike. His name stays on the roll.
A small asterisk is added beside it. The asterisk has no note.
A hundred years from now, if anyone opens this roll, they will be puzzled by that asterisk.
Being puzzled is the correct response.

### [阵-164] temple_schism · 选项1 失败

你说完理由，铁锤派一桌人同时站了起来。
「路是真的？路是谁铺的？是你吗？你也有假膝盖？」
你被淹没在质问里。投票结果：四十一擦，三十九留。他的名字被抹去了。
长老收起名录的时候，你看见他的手在抖。
【新文】
You finish your reason and the whole Hammer table stands up at once.
“The road was real? Who laid the road? You? Do you have false knees too?”
You go under in the questioning. The vote: forty-one to strike, thirty-nine to keep. His name is wiped.
As the elder puts the roll away, you see his hand shaking.

### [阵-166] temple_schism · 选项2 成功

四十一擦，三十九留。名字被一笔粗墨划去。

【新文】
Your voice is steadier than you expected. “If pure blood admits an exception, it stops being pure blood.
He knew that. He chose the knees, and he chose the cost.”
Forty-one to strike, thirty-nine to keep. The name goes out under one thick stroke of ink.
After it breaks up, an old believer stands behind you for a long time.
In the end he does not speak to you. He sets the white candle in his hand down by the door and goes.

但你在说完之后意识到它很重要。
【新文】
The instant the words stops being pure blood are out of your mouth, your own voice sounds like somebody else’s.
You are speaking for the doctrine, not for yourself. That distinction ought not to matter.
But once you have finished you understand that it matters a great deal.

### [阵-168] temple_schism · 选项3

### [阵-169] temple_schism · 选项3 成功
【原文】
长老的眼神复杂到你读不完。但他站了起来。
「好。」他只说了一个字。

他带着全体信众走进地下室。打开三道锁。
玻璃柜里那只金属手，被六十个火把照亮。
长老的解说很短：「建殿时出土。比城老。合金成分未知。」

他没有说它意味着什么。他不需要说。
六十个人站在一只金属手面前，同时理解了一件事：
他们守了两百年的教义，可能从第一个字就是错的。

投票在沉默中进行。传教士的名字以六十票留，零票擦。
没有人投擦。不是因为原谅了膝盖。
是因为那只手比膝盖老了两百年，而他们刚刚才知道。
【新文】
There is more in the elder’s eyes than you can read to the end. But he stands.
“All right.” One word.

He takes the whole congregation down into the basement. Opens three locks.
The metal hand in the glass case is lit by sixty torches.
The elder’s explanation is short: “Excavated when the Temple was built. Older than the city. Alloy unidentified.”

He does not say what it means. He does not need to.
Sixty people stand in front of a metal hand and understand one thing at the same moment:
the doctrine they have kept for two hundred years may have been wrong from its first word.

The vote proceeds in silence. The preacher’s name stays, sixty to nothing.
Nobody votes to strike. Not because the knees have been forgiven.
Because that hand is two hundred years older than the knees, and they have only just found out.

投票继续。你弃权了。最终四十擦，三十九留，一弃。
【新文】
The elder does not move. “That is not yours to do.” His voice is a door closing.
There are only three keys to the vault. You are not a Lampkeeper.
The vote goes ahead. You abstain. Final count: forty to strike, thirty-nine to keep, one abstention.

### [阵-174] hammer_forge · 事件正文
【原文】
铁锤派的地下锻造间。三台工业冲床，一座焦炭炉，墙上挂满了撬棍和短锤。
新来的人都要亲手锻一根自己的撬棍——「你的武器得认识你的手。」

铁匠是个六十多岁的老头，双手全是烧伤疤。指纹烧没了，握力还在——
他递给你一根生铁坯料的时候，你能感到那只手的力量和温度。
温度不对。太均匀了。活人的手心应该比手背热，他的整只手是同一个温度。

炉火很亮。你没有多看他的手。

「打。打到你满意为止。撬棍直了才能上阵。」
【新文】
The Hammer’s underground forge. Three industrial presses, one coke furnace, walls hung with pry bars and short hammers.
Everyone who joins has to forge their own pry bar by hand—“your weapon has to know your hands.”

The smith is an old man past sixty, both hands covered in burn scars. The fingerprints are burned off; the grip is not—
when he hands you the pig-iron billet you can feel the strength and the heat of that hand.
The heat is wrong. Too even. A living palm should be warmer than the back of the hand; his whole hand is one temperature.

The furnace is bright. You do not look at his hands for long.

“Beat it. Beat it until you are satisfied. A pry bar has to be straight before it goes out.”

### [阵-175] hammer_forge · 选项1
【原文】
认真锻打。跟着铁匠的节奏，一锤一锤地来。
【新文】
Forge it properly. Follow the smith’s rhythm, one stroke at a time.

### [阵-176] hammer_forge · 选项1 成功
【原文】
铁匠在你身后看了三十锤，没说话。第三十一锤的时候，
他把你的手腕往下压了两寸：「这里。力传到这里，铁才听话。」
你打了整整一个下午。撬棍出炉的时候还烫着，铁匠拿水淬了一下，
嘶的一声——那声音像在给这根铁定了性。

「不错。」他只说了这两个字。但他说的时候活动了一下手指，
关节发出了金属不该发出的声音。或者说，骨头不该发出的声音。
【新文】
The smith watches thirty strokes from behind you without a word. On the thirty-first
he presses your wrist down two inches: “Here. The force has to arrive here before the iron will listen.”
You work the whole afternoon. The bar is still hot when it comes off, and the smith quenches it,
one hiss—and the sound is like a verdict on what the iron is now.

“Not bad.” Two words, nothing more. But as he says them he works his fingers,
and the joints make a sound metal should not make. Or rather, a sound bone should not make.

### [阵-177] hammer_forge · 选项1 失败
【原文】
第十七锤打歪了。铁坯弯了一个角度，铁匠接过去，三锤正回来。
你看他那三锤——快得不像六十岁的人，准得不像烧伤过的手。

「明天再来。」他把你的弯撬棍扔回炉子里。生铁重新变红，
像一个被收回去的承诺。
【新文】
The seventeenth stroke goes crooked. The billet bends at an angle; the smith takes it and straightens it in three strokes.
You watch those three strokes—too fast for a man of sixty, too accurate for hands that have been burned.

“Come back tomorrow.” He throws your bent bar back into the furnace. The iron goes red again,
like a promise being taken back.

### [阵-178] hammer_forge · 选项2

### [阵-179] hammer_forge · 选项2 成功
【原文】
铁匠盯着你看了五秒钟。然后他笑了。
「小聪明。」他从墙上取下两根撬棍，扔在地上。
「左边的是模具铸的。右边的是手打的。你分得出来吗？」
你分不出来。

「我也分不出来。」他的声音忽然很轻。
「但锤子分得出来。手打的撬棍，每一根都有锻打者的手劲走向。
用的时候顺手，就像……」他停了一下，「就像它是你身上长出来的。」

你没有指出他话里的矛盾。铁锤派最恨「长在身上的机械」。
但一根认识你手劲的铁棍，和一只认识你神经的义手，区别在哪里？
【新文】
The smith looks at you for five seconds. Then he laughs.
“Clever.” He takes two pry bars off the wall and throws them on the ground.
“The left one is cast. The right one is hand-forged. Can you tell?”
You cannot.

“Neither can I.” His voice drops suddenly.
“But the hammer can. Every hand-forged bar carries the direction of the force that made it.
It sits right in your hand when you use it, like…” he stops, “like it grew out of you.”

You do not point out the contradiction. What the Hammer hates most is machinery that grew into somebody.
But what is the difference between an iron bar that knows your grip and a prosthetic hand that knows your nerves?

打得很糟。但你想了一整晚他没回答的问题。
【新文】
“People who ask that usually can’t forge.”
The smith is not angry; he simply stops paying attention to you. You stand holding the billet for a while, then start.
You do it badly. But you think all night about the question he did not answer.

### [阵-182] hammer_forge · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你一边打铁，一边用余光看他的手。
烧伤疤底下，食指和中指的弯曲弧度完全相同——肉手做不到这么对称。
无名指在握锤的时候有一个极细微的延迟，像是信号要多走半寸路。
他的手是修过的。也许不止修过。

你没有说。你继续打铁。
铁锤派的铁匠用一双不完全是肉的手，锻造着砸向义体的撬棍。
这件事如果说出去，能拆掉半个铁锤派。
所以你不说。你把这个发现和撬棍一起收好。
【新文】
You forge, watching his hands out of the corner of your eye.
Under the burn scars, the index and middle fingers curve at exactly the same angle—flesh cannot be that symmetrical.
The ring finger has a very small delay when he grips the hammer, as though the signal has half an inch further to travel.
His hands have been repaired. Perhaps more than repaired.

You say nothing. You keep forging.
The Hammer’s smith forges the pry bars that come down on prosthetics, with hands that are not entirely flesh.
Said out loud, this would take down half of the Hammer.
So you do not say it. You put the discovery away together with your pry bar.

### [阵-187] hammer_recruit · 事件正文
【原文】
铁锤派的入会仪式很简单：过金属探测门。
门是从海关淘汰下来的，灵敏度调到了最高——一颗纽扣大的金属都能让它尖叫。

新兵排成一列。脱掉所有金属饰品，赤脚，只穿单衣。
前面九个人都过了。安静。干净。

第十个人走进去，门叫了。
他愣住了。领队走过来，手持探测棒，从头扫到脚。棒在左胸停住了。

「心脏起搏器。」他的声音很小。「七岁装的。先天性心律不齐。不装就活不到八岁。」
他把单衣领口拉开——左胸有一道淡粉色的旧疤。
「我来铁锤派，是因为我知道被迫装上一个不属于自己的东西是什么感觉。
我不想让更多人经历这个。」
【新文】
The Hammer’s initiation is simple: walk through a metal detector.
The gate is a cast-off from customs, sensitivity turned all the way up—a piece of metal the size of a button will make it scream.

The recruits line up. All jewelry off, barefoot, single layer of clothing.
The first nine pass. Quiet. Clean.

The tenth walks in and the gate sounds.
He freezes. The captain comes over with a wand and sweeps him head to foot. The wand stops at the left side of the chest.

“Pacemaker.” His voice is small. “Fitted at seven. Congenital arrhythmia. Without it I wouldn’t have reached eight.”
He pulls his collar open—there is an old pale-pink scar on the left of his chest.
“I came to the Hammer because I know what it is to have something that isn’t yours forced into you.
I don’t want more people to go through it.”

### [阵-188] hammer_recruit · 选项1
【原文】
替他说话：「七岁的孩子没有选择权。誓约惩罚的是选择，不是命运。」
【新文】
Speak for him: “A seven-year-old has no right of choice. The covenant punishes choices, not fate.”

### [阵-189] hammer_recruit · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你的声音在队列里很突兀。但你说完之后，有三个人点了头。
领队看了你很久。然后他关掉了金属探测门。

「今天这扇门故障了。」他说。他走到新兵面前，拍了一下他的肩：
「别让我后悔。」

新兵进了铁锤派。他的心脏在左胸跳着，每一下都带着七岁时的金属回声。
你不知道这算破例还是正义。
但你知道领队关门的时候，手在抖。
【新文】
Your voice is jarring in the formation. But when you have finished, three people nod.
The captain looks at you a long time. Then he switches the metal detector off.

“The gate is faulty today,” he says. He walks up to the recruit and claps his shoulder:
“Don’t make me regret it.”

The recruit joins the Hammer. His heart beats on the left of his chest, every beat carrying a metal echo from when he was seven.
You do not know whether that counts as an exception or as justice.
But you know that the captain’s hand was shaking when he shut the gate off.

### [阵-190] hammer_recruit · 选项1 失败
【原文】
「命运？」领队转向你。「你知道多少人用这个借口？
工伤是命运。欠债是命运。被切了腿的流浪狗也是命运。
门开着的时候，什么都是命运。门关上了，才有誓约。」

新兵被带走了。你不知道他后来去了哪里。
但你知道领队说的也是对的。这就是最坏的部分——他说的也是对的。
【新文】
“Fate?” The captain turns on you. “Do you know how many people use that word?
A work injury is fate. A debt is fate. A stray dog with its leg cut off is fate.
While the gate is open, everything is fate. Only when the gate shuts is there a covenant.”

The recruit is taken away. You do not know where he went.
But you know the captain is also right. That is the worst part—he is also right.

### [阵-191] hammer_recruit · 选项2
【原文】
沉默。规矩是规矩。金属探测门不问原因。
【新文】
Say nothing. Rules are rules. A metal detector does not ask why.

### [阵-192] hammer_recruit · 选项2 结果
【原文】
队列继续。新兵被两个老成员架出去了。
门口没有吵闹。他走的时候回头看了一眼——不是看领队，
是看那扇金属探测门。

仪式结束后，你路过门口。门还开着，红色指示灯一闪一闪。
你从门中间走过去。门没有响。
你是干净的。这个事实今天没有让你高兴。
【新文】
The line moves on. Two senior members carry the recruit out.
There is no shouting at the door. As he goes he looks back once—not at the captain,
at the metal detector.

After the ceremony you pass the doorway. The gate is still on, the red indicator blinking.
You walk through it. It does not sound.
You are clean. Today that fact does not make you happy.

### [阵-193] hammer_recruit · 选项3

【新文】
Propose it: take the pacemaker out. If he means it, let him prove it to everyone.

### [阵-194] hammer_recruit · 选项3 成功
【原文】
新兵的脸白了一瞬——然后他点了头。「行。」
手术在铁锤派的简易医务室做的。没有麻醉——他自己要求的。
「我要记住这个痛。」

起搏器取出来的时候，他的心跳乱了三秒钟。
三秒钟之后，他自己的心脏找回了节奏——二十年来第一次不靠金属跳动。
他走过金属探测门。安静。干净。

领队把起搏器钉在了战利品架上。
你看着那个小小的金属盒子——它替一颗心脏跳了二十年。
现在它被钉在墙上，和砸来的义肢放在一起。
你分不出来哪些是「罪」，哪些是「药」。
【新文】
The recruit’s face goes white for an instant—then he nods. “All right.”
The surgery is done in the Hammer’s makeshift infirmary. No anesthetic—he asks for that himself.
“I want to remember this pain.”

When the pacemaker comes out his heartbeat scatters for three seconds.
After three seconds his own heart finds the rhythm again—beating for the first time in twenty years without metal.
He walks through the metal detector. Quiet. Clean.

The captain nails the pacemaker to the trophy rack.
You look at that small metal box—it beat for a heart for twenty years.
Now it is nailed to a wall among the prosthetics they smashed off people.
You cannot tell which of these are sins and which are medicine.

### [阵-195] hammer_recruit · 选项3 失败
【原文】
你的提议还没说完，三个老成员同时转过头来看你。
其中一个的目光很复杂：「你知道取出起搏器可能要他的命吧？」
你知道。你的提议就是建立在这个可能性上的。
这让你和领队之间产生了一种微妙的共鸣——你也愿意为了纯血赌命。
但共鸣维持了三秒就散了。因为赌的不是你的命。
【新文】
Before you have finished proposing it, three senior members turn to look at you at once.
There is a great deal in the eyes of one of them: “You know taking that out could kill him?”
You know. Your proposal was built on that possibility.
Which produces a strange resonance between you and the captain—you too are willing to gamble a life on pure blood.
The resonance lasts three seconds and goes. Because the life being gambled is not yours.

### [阵-196] hammer_recruit · 回响1
【原文】
铁锤派集会的时候，角落里有一个人的心跳声比别人大一点。
金属探测门从那天起就没再调回最高灵敏度。没人提过为什么。
【新文】
At the Hammer’s assembly there is one person in the corner whose heartbeat is a little louder than everyone else’s.
The metal detector has never been turned back to maximum sensitivity since that day. Nobody has mentioned why.

### [阵-197] hammer_recruit · 回响2
【原文】
战利品架上，所有义肢之间夹着一个小小的金属盒子。
新来的人问那是什么。老成员说：「那是一颗心。」新人以为是比喻。
【新文】
On the trophy rack, among all the prosthetics, there is a small metal box.
New arrivals ask what it is. The senior members say: “That is a heart.” The new ones assume it is a figure of speech.

### [阵-198] hammer_dawn · 事件正文
【原文】
天亮了。昨晚砸的那间黑诊所，领队让你回去「收尾」——
确认设备全毁了，没留下能用的零件。

你推开诊所半脱落的门。地上全是碎玻璃和砸烂的手术台残骸。
消毒水和焦糊味混在一起。墙角有一排铁柜，柜门被撬开了，里面空的——
但柜子底部有几道新鲜的拖痕。
有人比你们先到了一步。柜子里的东西在你们来之前就被搬走了。

手术台旁边的地上，有一只被踩碎的病历夹。
夹子里的病历不在了。只剩封面上半截名字和一行字：
「术后六小时翻身一次。」
【新文】
It is light. The captain sends you back to the back-room clinic you smashed last night to “close it out”—
confirm every piece of equipment is destroyed and no usable parts are left.

You push open the clinic’s half-hanging door. The floor is broken glass and the wreck of an operating table.
Disinfectant and burnt smell mixed together. Along one wall is a row of steel cabinets, doors pried open, empty inside—
but there are fresh drag marks along the base of them.
Somebody got here before you did. What was in those cabinets was carried out before you ever arrived.

On the floor beside the operating table is a crushed patient folder.
The notes are gone. All that is left is half a name on the cover and one line:
“Turn every six hours post-op.”

### [阵-199] hammer_dawn · 选项1
【原文】
按命令收尾——把剩余的东西全砸了，拍照交差。
【新文】
Follow the order. Smash everything left, photograph it, report in.

### [阵-200] hammer_dawn · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你用了一个小时把剩下的东西全部砸碎。最后一件是一台心率监护仪，
屏幕裂了，但指示灯还在闪。你拔掉电源的时候，指示灯最后亮了一下——绿的。
正常心率的颜色。

你拍了照，发给领队。领队回了一个字：「好。」
收工。任务完成。你的撬棍上沾着消毒水。
【新文】
You spend an hour breaking everything that is left. The last item is a cardiac monitor,
screen cracked, indicator still blinking. As you pull the plug the indicator flares one last time—green.
The color of a normal heart rate.

You take the photographs and send them to the captain. The captain replies with one word: “Good.”
Done. Task complete. There is disinfectant on your pry bar.

### [阵-201] hammer_dawn · 选项1 失败
【原文】
你砸最后一个柜子的时候，柜子后面掉出来一张照片。
医生和一个小女孩的合影。女孩的右腿是假的，但她在笑。
你把照片塞回碎玻璃里面，继续砸。
照片碎了。笑没碎。笑留在你脑子里了。
【新文】
As you break the last cabinet, a photograph falls out from behind it.
The doctor and a small girl. The girl’s right leg is false, and she is laughing.
You push the photograph back into the broken glass and go on smashing.
The photograph breaks. The laugh does not. The laugh stays in your head.

### [阵-202] hammer_dawn · 选项2
【原文】
去追那些拖痕。有人在你们动手之前就知道消息了。
【新文】
Follow the drag marks. Somebody knew before you moved.

### [阵-203] hammer_dawn · 选项2 成功
【原文】
拖痕通向后巷，后巷通向一条你不认识的暗道。暗道的尽头是码头。
码头上什么都没有了。但地面是湿的——海水的湿法和雨水不同，
海水干了以后会留盐。

有人从海上来，把诊所里最重要的东西在你们到之前运走了。
铁锤派砸的不是一间完整的诊所。铁锤派砸的是一间被掏空的壳。

你开始想：是谁给灰港通的风？
名单上有四十个人。这个问题一旦问出口，就会有四十种答法。
你把它咽了回去。但拖痕不会说谎。
【新文】
The drag marks lead to the back lane, the back lane to a passage you do not know. The passage ends at the docks.
There is nothing on the docks. But the ground is wet—seawater dries differently from rain,
and when seawater dries it leaves salt.

Somebody came in from the sea and moved the important things out of that clinic before you arrived.
What the Hammer smashed was not a working clinic. What the Hammer smashed was a hollowed-out shell.

You start to wonder: who tipped off Ashport?
There are forty names on the roster. Ask that question out loud and you will get forty kinds of answer.
You swallow it. But drag marks do not lie.

### [阵-204] hammer_dawn · 选项2 失败
【原文】
拖痕在后巷被雨水冲断了。你跟到路口，三个方向，
地上全是湿的，分不出哪个是拖痕哪个是积水。
你站在路口淋了十分钟的雨。回去复命的时候，领队问你怎么湿了。
你说下雨了。他信了。
【新文】
The drag marks break off in the back lane where the rain washed them out. You follow to the junction—three directions,
the ground wet everywhere, no telling which is a drag mark and which is standing water.
You stand at the junction in the rain for ten minutes. When you report back, the captain asks why you are wet.
You say it was raining. He believes you.

### [阵-205] hammer_dawn · 选项3
【原文】
捡起那半截病历封面。「术后六小时翻身一次」——这是写给活人的。
【新文】
Pick up the half-cover of the folder. “Turn every six hours post-op”—that was written for someone alive.

### [阵-206] hammer_dawn · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你蹲在碎玻璃里，把那半截封面翻过来。背面有铅笔写的几个字，
字迹很轻，像写的人怕被别人看见：
「疼的时候想想海。海是不疼的。」

你把封面折好，放进口袋。领队来收尾的时候你站在门口，
口袋里多了一张纸。
铁锤派的任务报告不需要提到这张纸。你也没提。
但那行字你记住了。海是不疼的。
——你不确定这是写给病人的，还是写给医生自己的。
【新文】
You crouch in the broken glass and turn the half-cover over. There are a few words on the back in pencil,
very light, as though whoever wrote them was afraid of being seen:
“When it hurts, think about the sea. The sea doesn’t hurt.”

You fold the cover and put it in your pocket. When the captain comes to close out, you are standing at the door
with one extra piece of paper on you.
A Hammer mission report does not need to mention that paper. You do not mention it.
But you remember the line. The sea doesn’t hurt.
—You are not sure whether it was written for the patient, or by the doctor for himself.

### [阵-207] hammer_dawn · 回响1
【原文】
拖痕的方向你认识。箱子是朝码头方向拖的。
那些空柜子的摆法、间距、甚至柜门上的锁型号——你在另一个地方见过一模一样的。
【新文】
You know which way those drag marks point. The crates were dragged toward the docks.
The way those empty cabinets were arranged, the spacing, even the model of lock on the doors—you have seen exactly this somewhere else.

### [阵-208] hammer_dawn · 回响2
【原文】
你路过一个码头。地上的盐渍已经被雨洗了好几遍了，
但你还是能看出拖痕的方向——朝海的那边。
有些东西被搬走之后，痕迹比东西活得久。
【新文】
You pass a dock. The salt on the ground has been washed by rain several times over,
but you can still make out which way the drag marks went—toward the sea.
Some things outlive themselves in the marks they leave when they are carried away.

### [阵-209] hammer_dawn · 回响3
【原文】
铁锤派的战报里有一个地址。地址旁边有人批注：
「到场时已空。设备全无。可能提前转移。」
铁锤砸了一面空墙。墙后面的人已经在海上了。
【新文】
There is an address in the Hammer’s action report. Somebody has annotated it:
“Empty on arrival. No equipment. Possible advance transfer.”
The hammer came down on a bare wall. The people behind the wall were already at sea.

### [阵-210] hammer_trophies · 事件正文
【原文】
铁锤派的地下室有一面墙。
墙上钉满了从黑诊所砸来的义体零件。液压臂、光学眼球、钛合金脊椎节段、
成排的人工关节——像猎人的鹿角墙，但钉着的不是鹿角。

领队每周带新人来参观：「每一件都是证据。证明这座城里有多少人
被骗着放弃了自己的身体。」

今天你被安排整理这面墙。给每件战利品编号、登记来源诊所。
整理到第四十七件——一只右手——的时候，你看见了手腕内侧的编码。
编码后四位是灰港的区域前缀。你认识这个编号体系。
【新文】
There is a wall in the Hammer’s basement.
It is covered in prosthetic parts taken off back-room clinics. Hydraulic arms, optical eyeballs, titanium spinal segments,
rows of artificial joints—like a hunter’s wall of antlers, except what is nailed up is not antlers.

Every week the captain brings the new people down to look: “Every one of these is evidence. Evidence of how many people in this city
were talked into giving up their own bodies.”

Today you have been assigned to sort the wall. Number each trophy, log which clinic it came from.
At the forty-seventh item—a right hand—you see the code on the inside of the wrist.
The last four digits are an Ashport district prefix. You know this numbering system.

### [阵-211] hammer_trophies · 选项1
【原文】
照实登记编码。灰港的前缀会引来一场追查。
【新文】
Log the code as it is. An Ashport prefix will bring an investigation down.

### [阵-212] hammer_trophies · 选项1 成功
【原文】
你把编码原样写进登记表。三天后，领队拿着表来找你。
「这个前缀……你知道对应哪里吗？」
你说不知道。他信了——或者他假装信了。

一周后铁锤派制定了新的夜袭路线，方向是码头。
你不知道那条路线的尽头是什么。但你知道那只右手的编码，
像一根引线，正在燃烧。
【新文】
You write the code into the register exactly as it stands. Three days later the captain comes to you with the register.
“This prefix… do you know what it corresponds to?”
You say you don’t. He believes you—or he acts as though he does.

A week later the Hammer draws up a new raid route, heading for the docks.
You do not know what is at the end of that route. But you know that the code on that right hand
is a fuse, and it is burning.

### [阵-213] hammer_trophies · 选项1 失败
【原文】
你登记完拿给领队过目。他翻到第四十七行，手指停了两秒。
「这个编码是你查过的？」你说是。
「以后别查。编码是诊所的事。我们只管砸。」
他把登记表收走了。你不知道他是不认识那个前缀，还是太认识了。
【新文】
You finish the log and take it to the captain to look over. He turns to line forty-seven and his finger stops for two seconds.
“Did you look this code up?” You say you did.
“Don’t, in future. Codes are the clinics’ business. Ours is smashing.”
He takes the register away. You do not know whether he did not recognize the prefix, or recognized it far too well.

### [阵-214] hammer_trophies · 选项2
【原文】
登记的时候把后四位改掉。灰港的事不该从这面墙上暴露。
【新文】
Alter the last four digits when you log it. Ashport should not be exposed off this wall.

### [阵-215] hammer_trophies · 选项2 成功
【原文】
你把后四位改成了一个不存在的前缀。
登记表上那行编码变成了无意义的噪声。灰港的线索消失在你的笔迹里。

整理完墙面，你洗了手。手上的铁锈洗得掉，
但你改掉四个数字的事实洗不掉。
你在铁锤派的地下室，替灰港保了一个秘密。
两个本不该有交集的世界，在你的登记表上偷偷握了一次手。
【新文】
You change the last four digits to a prefix that does not exist.
That line of the register becomes meaningless noise. The trail to Ashport disappears into your handwriting.

When the wall is sorted you wash your hands. The rust comes off.
The fact that you altered four digits does not.
In the basement of the Hammer, you have kept a secret for Ashport.
Two worlds that were never supposed to meet shook hands, quietly, on your register.

### [阵-216] hammer_trophies · 选项2 失败
【原文】
改到一半，旁边有人走过来。你来不及写完，字迹留了一个犹豫的弧度。
他没看你在写什么。但那个弧度留在纸上了，像一个没收回的手势。
你只能希望以后查表的人注意力不在第四十七行。
【新文】
Halfway through the change somebody walks past. You cannot finish the stroke, and the writing keeps a hesitating curve in it.
He does not look at what you are writing. But that curve stays on the paper, like a gesture that was never taken back.
All you can do is hope that whoever reads the register next is not looking at line forty-seven.

### [阵-217] hammer_trophies · 选项3
【原文】
把那只右手从墙上取下来，翻过来，看看掌心。
【新文】
Take that right hand off the wall, turn it over, and look at the palm.

### [阵-218] hammer_trophies · 选项3 结果
【原文】
你把那只右手从钉子上取下来。比想象的重——液压关节加上合金骨架。
翻过来。

掌心的硅胶皮层磨薄了一块。透过那块薄处，你能看见底下的线路板。
线路板上焊了一行极小的字——不是出厂编码，是手写的，
有人拿焊笔一个一个字烫上去的：

「给我女儿。她需要一只能翻书页的手。」

你把右手放回墙上。钉好。
那行字朝墙，没有人再会看见。
但你看见了。
【新文】
You lift the right hand off its nail. Heavier than you expected—hydraulic joints on an alloy frame.
Turn it over.

A patch of the silicone skin on the palm has worn thin. Through the thin place you can see the board underneath.
There is a line of very small characters soldered onto the board—not a factory code. Handwritten,
burned on one character at a time with a soldering pen:

“For my daughter. She needs a hand that can turn pages.”

You put the right hand back on the wall. Nail it up.
That line faces the wall now. Nobody will ever see it again.
But you saw it.

### [阵-219] hammer_trophies · 回响1
【原文】
铁锤派的夜袭路线最近改了。新路线经过码头。
你不知道那只右手的编码最终把他们带到了哪里。
【新文】
The Hammer’s raid routes have changed lately. The new one goes past the docks.
You do not know where the code on that right hand finally took them.

### [阵-220] hammer_trophies · 回响2
【原文】
战利品墙上第四十七号的登记卡被人翻过。卡还在。
你改过的数字没人追问。也许是真的没人在意。
也许是有人在意，但选了同一种沉默。
【新文】
Somebody has been through the registration card for number forty-seven on the trophy wall. The card is still there.
Nobody has questioned the digits you changed. Maybe nobody really cares.
Maybe somebody cares, and chose the same kind of silence.

### [阵-221] hammer_trophies · 回响3
【原文】
战利品架旁边新加了一块牌子：「这些都是罪证。」
你盯着那块牌子，想起掌心那行字。证据和遗书之间，隔着谁的定义。
【新文】
A new sign has gone up beside the trophy rack: “All of this is evidence of a crime.”
You look at the sign and think of that line on the palm. Between evidence and a last testament there is only somebody’s definition.
